and choosing ON variables
x
, we may assume that
p_{2}(0,x_{0},w_{0},\xi)=\sum_{j}{c_{j}}\xi_{j}^{2}
for
0\neq c_{j}\in\mathbf{R}
. Choose
j
so that
\xi_{j}\neq 0
at
\xi_{0}
, thus in a conical neighborhood of
\Gamma_{0}
if
\varepsilon
is small enough. By an ON change of variables we may take
j=1
, then
c_{1}=-L_{11}(0,x_{0},w_{0})
. Letting
b(\xi)=\xi_{1}^{-1}
near
\Gamma_{0}\cap\left\{\,|\xi|\geq 1\,\right\}
we may extend
b(\xi)
to a symbol in
S^{-1}
so that
b(\xi)\gtrsim\langle\xi\rangle^{-1}
. Then
Pb(D)
has a symbol expansion with
p_{j}b\in S^{j-1}
and

p_{2}(v,x,w,\xi)b({\xi})=-\sum_{jk}L_{jk}(v,x,w)\xi_{j}B_{k}(\xi)

where
B_{k}(\xi)=\xi_{k}b({\xi})\in S^{0}
. Since
B_{j}(D)D_{k}=B_{k}(D)D_{j}
, we find from (
A.2
)–(
A.3
) that

Pb(D)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}A_{j}(v,x,w,D)D_{j}+A_{0}(v,x,w,D)

where
A_{0}=(p_{1}\partial+p_{0})b(D)
,
A_{1}(v,x,w,\xi)=-L_{11}(v,x,w)B_{1}(\xi)\neq 0
near
\Gamma_{0}
and
A_{j}\in S^{0}
is real valued when
j>0
. Observe that
B_{1}(\xi)=1
near
\Gamma_{0}\bigcap\left\{\,|\xi|\geq 1\,\right\}
so it may be extended to be equal to 1 everywhere modulo terms having wave front set outside
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}
. This gives that
A_{1}=-L_{11}\neq 0
near
(0,x_{0},w_{0})
thus we can extend
a=A_{1}
so that
0\neq a(v,x,w)\in C^{\infty}
.
Replacing
A_{j}
with
a^{-1}A_{j}
we find that
Pb(D)=a(v,x,w)Q\in\Psi^{1}
where the symbol of
Q
is equal to
\xi_{1}+\sum_{j>1}A_{j}(v,x,w,\xi)\xi_{j}+A_{0}(v,x,w,\xi)
modulo
\Psi^{-1}
and terms having wave front set outside
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}
.

To obtain that
A_{j}
is independent of
\xi_{1}
for
j>0
, we shall use the Malgrange preparation theorem. If
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime})
we find by homogeneity for small enough
\varepsilon_{0}>0
that

(A.11)

\xi_{1}+\sum_{j>1}A_{j}(v,x,w,\xi)\xi_{j}=q(v,x,w,\xi)\left(\xi_{1}+r(v,x,w,\xi^{\prime})\right)

in a conical neighborhood of
\Gamma_{0}
, where
q>0
is homogeneous and
r
is real, homogeneous of degree 1 and vanishes when
\xi^{\prime}=0
. Then we can extend
q>0
to a homogeneous symbol by a cut-off, observe that the symbols depend
C^{\infty}
on
v
.
This replaces
a
by
0\neq aq\in S^{0}
, and by using Taylor’s formula we find that
r(v,x,w,\xi^{\prime})=\sum_{j>1}r_{j}(v,x,w,\xi^{\prime})\xi_{j}
with
r_{j}
homogeneous in
\xi^{\prime}
.
This gives that
Pb(D)=aq(v,x,w,D)Q
where
Q
is equal to (
A.10
) modulo
\Psi^{0}
and terms having wave front set outside
\overline{\Gamma}_{0}
.
The composition
aq(v,x,w,D)
with
Q
also gives lower order terms in
\Psi^{0}
which can be included in
A_{0}
.

Now the term
A_{0}\in\Psi^{0}
of
Q
can be replaced by
aq(v,x,w,D)R_{0}
modulo
\Psi^{-1}
where the symbol
R_{0}=A_{0}/aq\in S^{0}
. To make the term
R_{0}
independent of
\xi_{1}
we may use Malgrange division theorem and homogeneity for small enough
\varepsilon_{0}>0
to obtain that

(A.12)

R_{0}(v,x,w,\xi)=q_{0}(v,x,w,\xi)\left(\xi_{1}+r(v,x,w,\xi^{\prime})\right)+r_{0}(v,x,w,\xi^{\prime})

in a conical neighborhood of
\Gamma_{0}
, where
q_{0}\in S^{-1}
and
r_{0}\in S^{0}
by homogeneity. Cutting of
q_{0}
we may replace
R_{0}(v,x,w,D)
with
q_{0}(v,x,w,D)Q+r_{0}(v,x,w,D_{x^{\prime}})
modulo
\Psi^{-1}
near
\Gamma_{0}
. Since
R_{0}\cong(1+q_{0})R_{0}
modulo
\Psi^{-1}
, we obtain (
A.9
)–(
A.10
) with
a
replaced by
aq(1+q_{0})
.
Cutting off
q_{0}
where
|\xi|\gg 1
only changes the operator with terms in
\Psi^{-\infty}
, but gives that
1+q_{0}>0
making
aq(1+q_{0})\neq 0
. The composition of
aq(v,x,w,D)
with
q_{0}(v,x,w,D)Q
will also give lower order terms in
\Psi^{-1}
which can be included in
R
together with any cut-off terms. This gives the proposition after putting
t=x_{1}
and
x=x^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition
A.4
shows that the linerarized equation
P(v,x,w,D)u=f
may after ON changes of variables be microlocally be reduced to the system

Q_{j}(v,x,w,D)u_{j}\cong a_{j}^{-1}(v,x,w,D)f_{j}

where
f_{j}=\varphi_{j}(D)f
with
\varphi_{j}
given by Remark
A.3
. Observe that
u\cong\sum_{j}b_{j}(D)u_{j}
where
u_{j}
also has to be microlocalized.

Now, the reduction and the calculus will give terms
S\in\Psi^{-\infty}
which have smooth kernels. The errors
Sf(x)=\int S(x,y)f(y)\,dy
can be made small if
f
has support in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
x_{0}
by cutting off the kernel
S
. Let
\phi_{\delta}(x)=\phi((x-x_{0})/\delta)
where
0<\delta\leq 1
and
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbf{R}^{n})
such that
0\leq\phi\leq 1
, has support where
|x|<2
and is equal to 1 when
|x|\leq 1
, so that
\phi(x/\delta)\in C_{0}^{\infty}(B_{x_{0},2\delta})
if
B_{x_{0},\delta}=\left\{\,x:|x-x_{0}|\leq\delta\,\right\}
.

Lemma A.5
.

Let
S(x,y)\in C^{\infty}
and

S_{\delta}(x,y)=\phi_{\delta}(x)S(x,y)\phi_{\delta}(y)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{x_{0},2\delta}\times B_{x_{0},2\delta})
.
The mapping
S_{\delta}:C^{\infty}\mapsto C_{0}^{\infty}(B_{x_{0},2\delta})
is given by
S_{\delta}f(x)=\int S_{\delta}(x,y)f(y)\,dy
, and for
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{x_{0},\delta})
we have
S_{\delta}f(x)=Sf(x)
when
|x|\leq\delta
.
For
\delta
small enough,
\operatorname{Id}+S_{\delta}
has the inverse
(\operatorname{Id}+S_{\delta})^{-1}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-S_{\delta})^{j}\cong\operatorname{Id}

modulo operators with kernels in
C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{x_{0},2\delta}\times B_{x_{0},2\delta})
.

Proof.

We may assume that
x_{0}=0
, clearly
S_{\delta}f(x)=Sf(x)
if
\phi_{\delta}f=f
and
\phi_{\delta}(x)=1
.
If
f\in C^{\infty}
then
L^{\infty}
norm is
\|S_{\delta}f\|_{\infty}\leq c_{n}2^{n}\delta^{n}\|S\|_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}
.
By induction we get

(A.13)

\|S_{\delta}^{j}f\|_{\infty}\leq c_{n}2^{n}\delta^{n}\|S_{\delta}\|_{\infty}\|S^{j-1}_{\delta}f\|_{\infty}\\
\leq c_{n}^{j}2^{jn}\delta^{jn}\|S\|^{j}_{\infty}\|f\|_{\infty}\qquad j>1

where the kernels of
S_{\delta}^{j}
are in
C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{0,2\delta}\times B_{0,2\delta})
. Thus the series
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(-S_{\delta})^{j}
converges in
L^{\infty}
if
c_{n}2^{n}\delta^{n}\|S\|_{\infty}<1
.
Derivation of the terms in the series will only give factors
O(\delta^{-1})
so the convergence is in
C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{0,2\delta}\times B_{0,2\delta})
.
Then the inverse
(\operatorname{Id}+S_{\delta})^{-1}=\operatorname{Id}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}(-S_{\delta})^{j}\cong\operatorname{Id}
modulo operators with kernels in
C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{x_{0},2\delta}\times B_{x_{0},2\delta})
.
∎

Next, we shall solve the microlocalized equations
Q_{j}u_{j}=a_{j}^{-1}f_{j}=a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}f
with
u_{j}=0
when
t=0
. Here
\varphi_{j}
is given by Remark
A.3
,
Q_{j}
is given by (
A.10
) near
\Gamma_{\xi_{j},\varepsilon}
with
0\neq a_{j}\in S^{0}
given by Proposition
A.4
, but we shall treat terms
R\in\Psi^{-1}
as perturbations.
In the case when
A_{j}\equiv 0
, we would then find that
Q_{j}u_{j}\cong D_{t}u_{j}=a_{j}^{-1}f_{j}
, which has the approximate solution
u_{j}\cong\int_{0}^{t}a_{j}^{-1}f_{j}\,dt
. By using Fourier integral operators one can reduce to this case.

We shall denote by
I^{k}
the classical Fourier integral operators of order
k
with homogenous phase functions and classical symbol expansions depending
C^{\infty}
on
v
and
w
.
But we shall also use operators
F\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{k})
which are FIO
F(t)\in I^{k}
in
x
depending
C^{\infty}
on
t
,
v
and
w
for
(t,x)\in\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}^{n-1}
. Observe that
\Psi
DO of order
k
in
x
depending
C^{\infty}
on
t
,
v
and
w
are also in
C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{k})
and that
C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{k})\subset I^{k}
. By multiplying
I^{k}
by
I^{m}
we obtain operators in
I^{k+m}
by Remark
A.9
.

As before, we shall use ON coordinates
(t,x)\in\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}^{n-1}
and suppress the dependence on
v
and
w
. But the operators will depend
C^{\infty}
on
v
and
w
having symbols and phase functions that are uniformly bounded if
v\in C^{\infty}
and
w\in\mathbf{R}^{m}
are bounded.

Proposition A.6
.

Assume that
Q=D_{t}+a_{1}(t,x,D_{x})+a_{0}(t,x,D_{x})
depend
C^{\infty}
on
v\in C^{\infty}
and
w\in\mathbf{R}^{m}
, where
a_{1}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},S^{1})
is real and homogeneous of degree 1 and
a_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},S^{0})
. Then there exist elliptic Fourier integral operators
F_{0}(t)
and
F_{1}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{0})
such that
F_{0}(t)F_{1}(t)\cong\operatorname{Id}
and
QF_{0}(t)\cong F_{0}(t)D_{t}
modulo
C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{-1})
.
If
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}
then we have that

(A.14)

u(t,x)=iF_{0}(t)\int_{0}^{t}F_{1}(s)f(s,x)\,ds=\mathbb{F}f(t,x)

solves the initial value problem

(A.15)

Qu=(\operatorname{Id}+S)f\in C^{\infty}\qquad u(0,x)\equiv 0

where
S\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{-1})
and
\mathbb{F}\in I^{0}
.
Here
F_{0}(t)
,
F_{1}(t)
and
\mathbb{F}
depend
C^{\infty}
on
v\in C^{\infty}
and
w
, and have wave front sets close to the diagonal when
|t|\ll 1
. In fact, the canonical transformations given by
F_{0}(t)
and
F_{1}(t)
maps bicharacteristics of
D_{t}+a_{1}
to
t
lines and vice versa.

Corollary A.7
.

If
c_{j}\in\Psi^{k}
,
j=1,\ 2
, and
\operatorname{\rm supp}c_{1}\bigcap\operatorname{\rm supp}c_{2}=\emptyset
, then
c_{1}F_{0}(t)F_{1}(s)c_{2}\in I^{-\infty}

having a smooth kernel for small enough
s
and
t
.

Proof.

It is a classical result that there exists elliptic Fourier integral operators
F_{0}(t)
and
F_{1}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{0})
with the properties in the proposition.
The construction of the homogeneous phase function of the FIO involves solving the Hamilton-Jacobi equations, which depend on the derivatives of the principal symbol
\tau+a_{1}
of
Q
and thus depend
C^{\infty}
on
v
and
w
. Then the amplitude is given by the transport equations which also depend on the lower order term
a_{0}
of
Q
modulo terms in
C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},S^{-1})
and thus depend
C^{\infty}
on
v\in C^{\infty}
and
w
.
For the approximate inverse, one takes the phase function for the inverse canonical relation and the inverse amplitude, which also depend
C^{\infty}
on
v
and
w
.

If
f\in C^{\infty}_{0}
and
u=iF_{0}(t)v
with
v=\int_{0}^{t}F_{1}(s)f(s,x)\,ds
as in (
A.14
), then we find

(A.16)

Qu=iQF_{0}(t)v=(iF_{0}(t)D_{t}+S_{0})v=F_{0}(t)F_{1}(t)f+S_{0}v\\
=f+S_{1}f+S_{0}v=f+S_{1}f+S_{0}\int_{0}^{t}F_{1}(s)f\,ds=f+Sf

where
S_{0}
,
S_{1}
and
S\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},I^{-1})
.
∎

The approximate solution
u
in (
A.14
) depends
C^{\infty}
on the data
f
and
v
, but we shall need stronger estimates. For that we shall use the
L^{2}
Sobolev norms:

(A.17)

\|\varphi\|^{2}_{(k)}=\|\langle D\rangle^{k}\varphi\|^{2}\qquad\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}\qquad\forall\,k\in\mathbf{R}

Then the continuity of
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m}
depend uniformly on
u\in C^{\infty}
in theses spaces according to the following result, were we shall suppress the parameter
w
.

Lemma A.8
.

If
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m_{1}}
and
b(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m_{2}}
depend
C^{\infty}
on
u(x)
then we find that
a(u,x,D)b(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m_{1}+m_{2}}
also depends
C^{\infty}
on
u(x)
.
There exists
\ell\in\mathbf{R}
so that for any
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{0}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u(x)\in C^{\infty}
and any
k\in\mathbf{R}
there exists
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.18)

\|a(u,x,D)\varphi\|_{(k)}\leq C_{k}(\|u\|_{(\ell)})\|\varphi\|_{(k)}\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}

There exists
\ell\in\mathbf{R}
so that for any
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m_{1}}
and
b(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m_{2}}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u(x)
and
k\in\mathbf{R}
there exists
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.19)

\|\,[a(u,x,D),b(u,x,D)]\varphi\|_{(k)}\leq C_{k}(\|u\|_{(\ell)})\|\varphi\|_{(k+m_{1}+m_{2}-1)}\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}

There exists
\ell\in\mathbf{R}
so that for any
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u(x)
having real valued symbol modulo
S^{m-1}
and any
k\in\mathbf{R}
there exists
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.20)

\|\operatorname{Im}a(u,x,D)\varphi\|_{(k)}\leq C_{k}(\|u\|_{(\ell)})\|\varphi\|_{(k+m-1)}\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}

where
2i\operatorname{Im}a(u,x,D)=a(u,x,D)-a^{*}(u,x,D)
depends
C^{\infty}
on
u
. Here the functions
C_{k}(t)
only depend on the seminorms of the symbols.

Proof.

First we note that by definition any seminorm of order
k\in\mathbf{N}
of
a(u,x,\xi)\in S^{m}
is bounded by
\|u\|_{C^{k}}
for some
k\in\mathbf{R}
. By the Sobolev embedding theorem, the
C^{k}
norm of
u
can be bounded by the norm
\|u\|_{(k+s)}
with
s>n/2
.

If
a(u,x,\xi)\in S^{m_{1}}
and
b(u,x,\xi)\in S^{m_{2}}
then
a(u,x,D)b(u,x,D)=c(u,x,D)
is given by

(A.21)

c(u,x,\xi)=e^{i\langle D_{\xi},D_{y}\rangle}a(u,x,\xi)b(u,y,\eta)\big|_{\begin{subarray}{c}y=x\\
\eta=\xi\end{subarray}}

The mapping
a,b\mapsto c
is weakly continuous on the symbol classes
S^{m}
so that any seminorm of
c
only depends on some seminorms of
a
and
b
, see
[
10
, Th. 18.4.10
′
]
.
(Weak continuity means that the restriction to a bounded set is continuous in the
C^{\infty}
topology, see
[
10
, Def. 18.4.9]
.)
Thus if
a(u,x,D)
and
b(u,x,D)
depend
C^{\infty}
on
u\in C^{\infty}
then
c(u,x,D)
also does.
Observe that the number of seminorms that is needed does not depend on the symbol classes
S^{m_{j}}
, it only depends on the dimension.

We may reduce the estimate (
A.18
) to the case
k=0
by replacing
a(u,x,D)
with
A(x,D)=\langle D\rangle^{k}a(u,x,D)\langle D\rangle^{-k}\in\Psi^{0}
and
\varphi
with
\langle D\rangle^{k}\varphi
. Here the symbol expansion of

A(x,\xi)\cong a(u(x),x,\xi)+kD_{x}a(u(x),x,\xi)\xi\langle\xi\rangle^{-2}+\dots+A_{j}(x,\xi)+\dots

where the term
A_{j}(x,\xi)\in S^{-j}
has the factor
D^{j}_{x}a(u(x),x,\xi)
,
\forall\,j
. The error term for the expansion
\sum\limits_{j=0}^{n}A_{j}(x,D)
is
r_{n}(x,D)\in\Psi^{-n-1}
, which has a
C^{0}
kernel which is bounded by
\|u\|_{C^{n+1}}
which gives a bound of
r_{n}(x,D)
on
L^{2}
. The
L^{2}
norm of
A_{j}(x,D)
is bounded by a fixed seminorm of
A_{j}(x,\xi)
,
\forall\,j
, see
[
10
, Th. 18.6.3]
. This seminorm in turn depends on a fixed seminorm of
a(u,x,\xi)
which gives (
A.18
) for some
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
.

Any seminorm of the symbol of the commutator
[a(u,x,D),b(u,x,D)]\in S^{m_{1}+m_{2}-1}
depends on the same seminorm of the symbols of the compositions
a(u,x,D)b(u,x,D)
and
b(u,x,D)a(u,x,D)
. These seminorms in turn depend on some seminorms of
a(u,x,\xi)
and
b(u,x,\xi)
. Thus we obtain (
A.19
) from (
A.18
) for some
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
.

If
a\in S^{m}
then the adjoint
a^{*}(u,x,D)
is given by

(A.22)

a^{*}(u,x,\xi)=e^{i\langle D_{\xi},D_{x}\rangle}\overline{a}(u,x,\xi)

which is weakly continuous in the symbol class
S^{m}
by
[
10
, Th. 18.1.7]
. If
a
is real modulo
S^{m-1}
then
\operatorname{Im}a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m-1}
. Thus any seminorm of the symbol of
\operatorname{Im}a(u,x,D)
is bounded by some seminorms of
a(u,x,\xi)
, giving (
A.20
) for some
C_{k}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
.
∎

Remark A.9
.

The results of Lemma
A.8
also holds for
a(u,x,D)\in\Psi^{m}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u
composed by
F\in I^{k}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u
, e.g., the FIO given by Proposition
A.6
. Operators in
I^{-\infty}
have smooth kernels which are
C^{\infty}
functions of
u
.

In fact, Theorem 9.1 in
[
8
]
shows that the conjugation of
\Psi
DO with FIO gives symbol expansions similar to (
A.21
) after change of variables, see for example (9.2)
′′

in
[
8
]
. This result is about Weyl operators, but by Theorem 4.5 in
[
8
]
it can be extended to operators having the Kohn-Nirenberg quantization. This gives a calculus with symbol expansions of classical homogeneous FIO with homogeneous phases and symbols, see pages 441–442 in
[
8
]
. For example, if
a\in\Psi^{m}
and
F\in I^{k}
then we have
\|aFu\|^{2}=\langle F^{*}a^{*}aFu,u\rangle
where
F^{*}a^{*}aF=b\in\Psi^{2(m+k)}
, and similar result holds for
\|Fau\|^{2}
.

For
S\in I^{-\infty}
the
C^{\infty}
dependence means that for any
k\in\mathbf{R}
we have
S\in I^{-k}
depending
C^{\infty}
on
u
. Since the kernel is obtained by taking the Fourier transform in
\xi
of the symbol, we find that the kernel of
S
is smooth and is a
C^{\infty}
function of
u
.

Next, we are going to prove estimates for the microlocalized operators.
Then we will use ON coordinates
(t,x)\in\mathbf{R}\times\mathbf{R}^{n-1}
and for
k\in\mathbf{R}
and
T>0
define the local norms

(A.23)

\|\varphi\|^{2}_{k,T}=\int_{|t|\leq T}\|\varphi\|_{k}^{2}(t)\,dt\qquad\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}

and
\|\varphi\|_{k,j,T}=\|\langle D_{t}\rangle^{j}\varphi\|_{k,T}
,
\forall\,j\in\mathbf{R}
, with
\|\varphi\|_{k}^{2}(t)=\int|\langle D_{x}\rangle^{k}\varphi(t,x)|^{2}\ dx
.

Proposition A.10
.

Let
v,\,f\in C^{\infty}_{0}
and
u\in C^{\infty}
be a solution to

(A.24)

Q(v,t,x,w,D)u=\partial_{t}u+\sum_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}(v,t,x,w,D_{x})\partial_{x_{j}}u\\
+A_{0}(v,t,x,w,D_{x})u=f\qquad u(0,x,w)=0

where
A_{j}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{0})
depends
C^{\infty}
on
v
,
\forall\,j
, and
A_{j}
is real valued modulo
S^{-1}
for
j>0
. Then there exists
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
so that for any
k\in\mathbf{N}
there exists
C_{k}(r)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.25)

\|\phi u\|_{(k)}^{2}\leq C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell})\|f\|_{(k)}^{2}

if
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}
has support where
|t|\leq 1
.
The estimate only depends on the seminorms of the symbol of
Q
and
\phi
.

Thus, for any
k\in\mathbf{N}
we get uniform local bounds on
\|\phi u\|_{(k)}
when
\|v\|_{(\ell)}
is uniformly bounded. Now
Q
is a differential operator in
t
but a
\Psi
DO in
x
, so in the proof we shall use Lemma
A.8
in the
x
variables.

Proof.

Let
A\partial_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}\partial_{x_{j}}
and
\langle u,u\rangle_{k}(t)=\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)
be the sesquilinear form,
then

(A.26)

\partial_{t}\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)=2\operatorname{Re}\langle\partial_{t}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)=2\operatorname{Re}\langle f,u\rangle_{k}(t)\\
-2\operatorname{Re}\langle A\partial_{t}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)-2\operatorname{Re}\langle A_{0}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)

Conjugating with
e^{-Ct}
gives

(A.27)

\partial_{t}(e^{-Ct}\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t))=e^{-Ct}\big(2\operatorname{Re}\langle f,u\rangle_{k}(t)\\
-2\operatorname{Re}\langle A\partial_{x}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)-2\operatorname{Re}\langle A_{0}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)-C\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)\big)

where

(A.28)

2\operatorname{Re}\langle A\partial_{x}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)=2\operatorname{Re}\langle[\langle D_{x}\rangle^{k},A]\partial_{x}\langle D_{x}\rangle^{-k}w,w\rangle_{0}(t)\\
+\langle[\operatorname{Re}A,\partial_{x},]w,w\rangle_{0}(t)+2\operatorname{Re}\langle i\operatorname{Im}A\partial_{x}w,w\rangle_{0}(t)=\langle R_{k}w,w\rangle_{0}(t)

where
[\operatorname{Re}A,\partial_{x}]
and
\operatorname{Im}A\partial_{x}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{0})
and
w=\langle D_{x}\rangle^{k}u
. The calculus gives that the operator
[\langle D_{x}\rangle^{k},A]\partial_{x}\langle D_{x}\rangle^{-k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{0})
, so that
R_{k}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{0})
depends
C^{\infty}
on
v
.

Since
\|w\|_{0}=\|u\|_{k}
we find by
using Lemma
A.8
that

(A.29)

|\langle R_{k}w,w\rangle_{0}(t)|\leq C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))\|w\|_{0}^{2}(t)=C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)

for some
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
and
C_{k}(r)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R})
, and clearly

(A.30)

|\langle f,u\rangle_{k}(t)|\leq\|f\|_{k}^{2}(t)+\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)

We also obtain from Lemma
A.8
that

(A.31)

|\langle A_{0}u,u\rangle_{k}(t)|\leq C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)

where in the following we will take the maximum of different
\ell
and
C_{k}(r)
.
Summing up, there exists
C_{k}(r)
so that for any
C>0
we have

(A.32)

\partial_{t}(e^{-Ct}\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t))\leq e^{-Ct}\left((C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))-C)\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)+C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))\|f\|_{k}^{2}(t)\right)

Now we may replace
C_{k}(r)
by a nondecreasing function
and put

C_{k}=\max_{|t|\leq 1}C_{k}(\|v\|_{\ell}(t))\leq C_{k}\left(\max_{|t|\leq 1}\|v\|_{\ell}(t)\right)

where
\|v\|_{\ell}(t)\leq C_{0}\|v\|_{(\ell+1)}
,
\forall\,t
, by Sobolev’s inequality.
Since
\|u\|_{k}(0)=0
we find by integrating that

(A.33)

e^{-C_{k}t}\|u\|_{k}^{2}(t)\leq e^{C_{k}}C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell+1)})\|f\|_{k,1}^{2}\qquad t\in[-1,1]

Integrating over
[-1,1]
we obtain that

(A.34)

\|u\|_{k,1}^{2}\leq 2e^{2C_{k}}C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell+1)})\|f\|_{k,1}^{2}

where
\|f\|_{k,1}\leq\|f\|_{(k)}
.
Renaming
2e^{2C_{k}}C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell+1)})
as
C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell+1)})
and changing
\ell

we obtain

(A.35)

\|u\|_{k,1}^{2}\leq C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell)})\|f\|^{2}_{(k)}\qquad\forall\,k\in\mathbf{N}

Next, we shall estimate

(A.36)

\|u\|_{k,j,1}^{2}\leq C_{k,j}(\|v\|_{(\ell)})\|f\|^{2}_{(k+j)}\qquad\forall\,k\in\mathbf{N}

when
j\in\mathbf{N}
. For that it suffices to estimate
\|D_{t}^{j}u\|_{k,1}

which we shall do by induction. The case
j=0
is given by (
A.35
), and we assume that (
A.36
) holds for
i\leq j
for some
j\in\mathbf{N}
. Since
Qu=\partial_{t}u+Au=f
with
A\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{1})
, we have

(A.37)

\|D_{t}^{j+1}u\|_{k,1}\leq\|D_{t}^{j}f\|_{k,1}+\|D_{t}^{j}Au\|_{k,1}

where
D_{t}^{j}A=AD_{t}^{j}+\sum_{0\leq i<j}B_{i}D_{t}^{i}

with
B_{i}(t,x,D_{x})\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R},\Psi^{1})
being a
\Psi
DO in
x
depending
C^{\infty}
on
t
and
v
. By using Lemma
A.8
and integrating over
[-1,1]
we find that

(A.38)

\|D_{t}^{j}Au\|_{k,1}\leq\sum_{0\leq i\leq j}C_{k,i}(\|v\|_{(\ell)})\|u\|_{k,i,1}

so the induction hypothesis gives (
A.36
) for any
j\in\mathbf{N}
.

Finally, we shall show that

(A.39)

\|\phi u\|^{2}_{(k)}\leq C_{k}(\|v\|_{(\ell)})\|f\|^{2}_{(k)}\qquad\forall\,k\in\mathbf{N}

if
\phi\in C^{\infty}
is supported where
|t|\leq 1
.
To estimate
\|\phi u\|_{(k)}^{2}
it suffices to estimate
\|D_{x}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{j}\phi u\|
for
|\alpha|+j\leq k
.
We have that

[D_{x}^{\alpha}D_{t}^{j},\phi]=\sum_{|\beta|+i\leq k}B_{\beta,i}D_{x}^{\beta}D_{t}^{i}

where
B_{\beta,i}\in C^{\infty}
has support where
|t|\leq 1
.
Thus, (
A.36
) gives that

(A.40)

\|D_{x}^{\beta}D_{t}^{j}\phi u\|\leq C\sum_{0\leq i\leq j}\|u\|_{k-i,i,1}\leq\sum_{0\leq i\leq j}C_{k-i,i}(\|v\|_{(\ell)})\|f\|^{2}_{(k)}

which completes the proof.
∎

Next, we shall solve the IVP for the linearized equation

(A.41)

P(v(x,w),x,w,\partial)u(x,w)=f(x,w)

where
f
and
v\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{m+n},\mathbf{R}^{n})
with
v(x_{0},w_{0})=0
and
P
is on the form (
A.2
) satisfying (
A.3
) and (
A.4
) with
u_{1}=0
.
In the following, we shall suppress the parameters
v
and
w
, the preparation will only depend on the bounds on these parameters.

To solve equation (
A.41
), we shall assume that
x_{0}=0
and use the microlocal normal forms given by Proposition
A.4
. In fact, for any small enough
\varepsilon>0
we can by Remark
A.3
find a partition of unity
\{\varphi_{j}(\xi)\}_{j}
with
\varphi_{j}\in S^{0}
supported in cones
\Gamma_{\xi_{j},\varepsilon}
and ON variables
(x_{1},x^{\prime})
so that
Pb_{j}=a_{j}Q_{j}+Rj
satisfies the conditions in Proposition
A.4
with
\Gamma_{0}=(0,0,w_{0})\times\Gamma_{\xi_{j},\varepsilon}
after the change of variables.
Here
0\neq a_{j}\in S^{0}
,
\langle\xi\rangle^{-1}\lesssim b_{j}(\xi)\in S^{-1}
and

Q_{j}=D_{x_{1}}+A_{j}D_{x^{\prime}}+A_{0,j}

satisfies the conditions in Proposition
A.6
. The operator
R_{j}\in\Psi^{1}
has symbol in
S^{-1}
in a conical neighborhood of
\Gamma_{0}
.
Ignoring the operator
R_{j}
, which will be handled as a perturbation, we obtain from Proposition
A.6

that if
f\in C^{\infty}
then
u_{j}=\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}f
solves

(A.42)

Q_{j}u_{j}=(\operatorname{Id}+s_{j})a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}f=(a_{j}^{-1}+r_{j})\varphi_{j}f\qquad u_{j}\big|_{t=0}=0

where
s_{j}
and
r_{j}\in I^{-1}
.

But
u_{j}
may not be localized near
\Gamma_{0}
.
To handle the localization and the error term
R_{j}
we
shall microlocalize
u_{j}
depending on parameters. We shall use the cut-off
\psi_{j,\varrho}(\xi)=\psi_{j}(\xi)\chi_{\varrho}(\xi)
given by Remark
A.3
with
\varrho\geq 1
such that
0\leq\chi_{\varrho}\leq 1
has support where
|\xi|\geq\varrho
,
\psi_{j}\varphi_{j}=\varphi_{j}
and
\operatorname{\rm supp}\psi_{j}\in\Gamma_{\xi_{j},\varepsilon}
. We shall make cut-offs with
\Phi(x)\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbf{R}^{n})
such that
0\leq\Phi\leq 1
,
\Phi
has support where
|x|\leq 1
and is equal to 1 when
|x|\leq 1/2
.
We find that

(A.43)

u_{j}=\psi_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}+(1-\psi_{j,\varrho})\Phi u_{j}+(1-\Phi)u_{j}=u_{j,\varrho}+S_{j,\varrho}f

when
|x|<1/2
, where
u_{j,\varrho}=\psi_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}
and
S_{j,\varrho}=(1-\psi_{j,\varrho})\Phi\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}\in I^{0}
since
\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}\in I^{0}
. Thus we find that
S_{j,\varrho}f(x)
depends on the values of
f(y)
when
y_{1}
is in the interval between 0 and
x_{1}
.
Since
\psi_{j}\varphi_{j}=\varphi_{j}
we find that
(1-\psi_{j,\varrho})\varphi_{j}=(1-\chi_{\varrho})\varphi_{j}
which is supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
.

By (
A.15
) we also have

Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}=[\psi_{j,\varrho},Q_{j}]\Phi\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}+(1-\psi_{j,\varrho})[Q_{j},\Phi]\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}\\
+(1-\psi_{j,\varrho})\Phi(\operatorname{Id}+S_{j})a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}\in I^{-\infty}

and also that
Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly with symbol supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
, and
S_{j}\in I^{-1}
.
Since
\Psi^{0}\ni a_{j}\neq 0
we have
a_{j}a_{j}^{-1}=\operatorname{Id}+B_{j}
with
B_{j}\in\Psi^{-1}
.
We find from (
A.9
), (
A.42
) and (
A.43
) that

(A.44)

Pb_{j}u_{j,\varrho}=a_{j}Q_{j}(u_{j}-S_{j,\varrho}f)+R_{j}u_{j,\varrho}\\
=\left((\operatorname{Id}+B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\varphi_{j}-a_{j}Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}+R_{j,\varrho}\right)f

when
|x|<1/2
, where
a_{j}Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}\in I^{-\infty}
,
a_{j}r_{j}\in I^{-1}
and
R_{j,\varrho}=R_{j}\psi_{j,\varrho}\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}\in I^{-1}
with symbol supported where
|\xi|\gtrsim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
when
|x|\ll 1
since the symbol
R_{j}\psi_{j,\varrho}\in S^{-1}
for
|x|\ll 1
by Proposition
A.4
.
Here
E\in I^{k}
in the open set
\Omega\subset T^{*}\mathbf{R}^{n}
means that
E=E_{0}+E_{1}
where
E_{1}\in I^{k}
and
\operatorname{WF}E_{0}\bigcap\Omega=\emptyset
.
Since
\varrho\psi_{j,\varrho}\in S^{1}
uniformly by Remark
A.3
, we find
\varrho R_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
|x|\ll 1
.

Now we define

(A.45)

u_{\varrho}(x)=\sum_{j}b_{j}(D)\psi_{j,\varrho}(D)\Phi u_{j}(x)=\sum_{j}b_{j}(D)u_{j,\varrho}(x)

where
\varrho b_{j}\psi_{j,\varrho}\in S^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
.
We obtain from (
A.44
) and (
A.45
) that

(A.46)

Pu_{\varrho}=f+\sum_{j}\left((B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\varphi_{j}+R_{j,\varrho}-a_{j}Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}\right)f=(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho})f

where
R_{\varrho}=\sum_{j}(B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\varphi_{j}+R_{j,\varrho}-a_{j}Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}\in I^{-1}
when
|x|\ll 1
. We shall localize the first terms in
\xi
by writing
B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j}=(B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})(1-\chi_{\varrho})+{(B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\chi_{\varrho}}
which gives

(A.47)

R_{\varrho}=R_{\varrho,0}+R_{\varrho,1}

with
R_{\varrho,0}=\sum_{j}(B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\varphi_{j}(1-\chi_{\varrho})-a_{j}Q_{j}S_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly with symbol supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
, and
R_{\varrho,1}=\sum_{j}(B_{j}+a_{j}r_{j})\varphi_{j,\varrho}+R_{j,\varrho}\in I^{-1}
uniformly and
\varrho R_{\varrho,1}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
and
|x|\ll 1
since
\varrho\chi_{\varrho}\in S^{1}
uniformly.

Since we are only need local solutions, we may cut off near
x=0
.
Let
\Phi_{\delta}(x)=\Phi(x/\delta)
with
0<\delta\leq 1
,
To solve the equation near
x=0
it is enough that
\Phi_{\delta}Pu_{\varrho}=\Phi_{\delta}f
for some
0<\delta\leq 1
.
If
f
has support where
|x|\leq\delta/2
then
we obtain from (
A.44
) that
\Phi_{\delta}Pu_{\varrho}=\Phi_{\delta}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho})\Phi_{\delta}f=(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho})f
where
R_{\delta,\varrho}=\Phi_{\delta}R_{\varrho}\Phi_{\delta}
.
By (
A.47
) we have

(A.48)

R_{\delta,\varrho}=R_{\delta,\varrho,0}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1}

with
R_{\delta,\varrho,j}=\Phi_{\delta}R_{\varrho,j}\Phi_{\delta}
.
For fixed
0<\delta\ll 1
we have
\varrho R_{\delta,\varrho,1}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
and
R_{\delta,\varrho,0}\in I^{0}
has
C^{\infty}
kernel depending on
\varrho
and
\delta
with symbol supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
.

It remains to invert the term
\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho}
in order solve equation (
A.41
).
This will be done in two steps, first making
R_{\delta,\varrho,1}
small by taking large enough
\varrho
. This may increase the seminorms of
R_{\delta,\varrho,0}
, but this term can then be made small by localizing in a sufficiently small neighborhood of
x=0
.

Since
\varrho R_{\delta,\varrho,1}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
, we find by Remark
A.9
that there exists
\varrho_{\delta}\geq 1
so that if
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
we have

\|R_{\delta,\varrho,1}f\|_{(0)}\leq\|f\|_{(0)}/2
for
f\in\mathcal{S}
.
Then we find that

(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}=\operatorname{Id}+\sum_{k>0}(-R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{k}\in I^{0}\ \text{ uniformly in $\varrho$}

Observe that
(-R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{k}
has kernel supported where
|x|\leq\delta
and
|y|\leq\delta
. If we then solve

(A.49)

Q_{j}u_{j}=a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}f\qquad u_{j}\big|_{x_{1}=0}=0

for
f
supported where
|x|\leq\delta/2
, then the previous reduction and (
A.48
) gives

(A.50)

\Phi_{\delta}Pu_{\varrho}=(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho})(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}f=(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,2})f

where
R_{\delta,\varrho,2}=R_{\delta,\varrho,0}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}\in I^{-\infty}
with
C^{\infty}
kernel supported where
|x|\leq\delta
and
|y|\leq\delta
and with symbol supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
. Observe that we have uniform bounds for fixed
\delta
when
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
and these bounds depend on the bounds on the symbol of
P
and the parameters
v\in C^{\infty}
and
w
. We shall later put more restraints on the lower bound of
\varrho
because of conditions on the estimates, see (
A.62
), and the values of
u_{\varrho}(0)
and
\partial u_{\varrho}(0)
, see (
A.66
).

Now we have to shrink the support of
R_{\delta,\varrho,2}
to lower the norm of the kernel without changing
R_{\delta,\varrho,1}
. With fixed
0<\delta\leq 1
and
\varrho_{\delta}\geq 1
, we assume
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
and multiply the equation (
A.50
) with
\Phi_{\delta_{0}}
with
0<\delta_{0}\leq\delta/2<1/2
so that
\Phi_{\delta}=1
on
\operatorname{\rm supp}\Phi_{\delta_{0}}
. If
f
is supported where
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2<1/4
, then we obtain as before that

(A.51)

\Phi_{\delta_{0}}Pu_{\varrho}=\left(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}\right)f

where
R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}=\Phi_{\delta_{0}}R_{\delta,\varrho,2}\Phi_{\delta_{0}}
.

Lemma A.11
.

There exists
C_{0}>0
so that if
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
and
0<\delta_{0}\leq\delta/2<1/2
we have

(A.52)

\|R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}\varphi\|_{(0)}\leq C_{0}\varrho^{n}\delta_{0}^{n}\|\varphi\|_{(0)}\qquad\forall\,\varphi\in\mathcal{S}

Proof.

Now
(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}\in I^{0}
uniformly in
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
so
R_{\delta,\varrho,2}=R_{\delta,\varrho,0}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}\in I^{-\infty}
having
C^{\infty}
kernel but also in
I^{0}
uniformly in
\varrho\geq 1
with bounded symbol supported where
|\xi|\lesssim\varrho
modulo
S^{-\infty}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
.

By integrating the symbol in
\xi
, we find that
R_{\delta,\varrho,2}
has
C^{\infty}
kernel
R_{2}(x,y)
so that
\|R_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq C\varrho^{n}
.
Thus
R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,0}
has kernel
R_{0}=\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(x)R_{2}(x,y)\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(y)\in I^{-\infty}
and since

\|R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}f\|^{2}_{(0)}\leq\int\left|\int R_{0}(x,y)f(y)\,dy\right|^{2}\,dx\leq\|R_{0}\|^{2}_{(0)}\|f\|^{2}_{(0)}

where
\|R_{0}\|^{2}_{(0)}\lesssim\varrho^{2n}(\int\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(y)\,dy)^{2}\lesssim\varrho^{2n}\delta_{0}^{2n}
, which proves the lemma.
∎

By Lemma
A.11
we find that if
c_{0}=(2C_{0})^{-1/n}
then for
0<\delta_{0}\leq\min(c_{0}/\varrho,\delta/2)
we have that
\|R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}(x,D)\varphi\|_{(0)}\leq\|\varphi\|_{(0)}/2
for
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
. We obtain that

(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}(x,D))^{-1}=\sum_{j\geq 0}(-R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}(x,D))^{j}\cong\operatorname{Id}

modulo an operator in
I^{-\infty}
with
C^{\infty}_{0}
kernel supported where
|x|\leq\delta_{0}
and
|y|\leq\delta_{0}
.
By replacing
f
in (
A.49
) by
(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2})^{-1}f
we obtain the first part of the following result.

Proposition A.12
.

Let
P
be given by Theorem
A.1
,
\varphi_{j}
be given by Remark
A.3
,
Pb_{j}=a_{j}Q_{j}+R_{j}
by Proposition
A.4
with
x_{0}=0
and let
R_{\delta,\varrho,1}
and
R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2}
be given by (
A.48
) and (
A.51
) depending
C^{\infty}
on
v
and
w
. Then there exist
0<\delta\leq 1
,
\varrho_{\delta}\geq 1
and
c_{0}>0
so that if
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
and
0<\delta_{0}\leq\min(c_{0}/\varrho,\delta/2)
, then
(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta,\varrho,1})^{-1}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\delta_{0},\varrho,2})^{-1}=\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}}\in I^{0}
is uniformly bounded.
If
f\in C^{\infty}
and
u_{j}\in C^{\infty}
solves

(A.53)

Q_{j}u_{j}=a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}})\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f\qquad u_{j}\big|_{x_{1}=0}=0\quad\forall\ j

then after ON changes of
x
variables we find that
u_{\varrho}(x)=\sum_{j}b_{j}\varphi_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}(x)
solves

(A.54)

Pu_{\varrho}=f

when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|\ll 1
. We also have that

(A.55)

u_{\varrho}(x)=c_{x,\varrho}(f)\quad\text{and}\quad\partial u_{\varrho}(x)=d_{x,\varrho}(f)

where
\varrho c_{x,\varrho}
and
d_{x,\varrho}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
are
o(1)
uniformly for
\varrho\to\infty
when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|\ll 1
, i.e.,
\varrho c_{x,\varrho}(f)
and
d_{x,\varrho}(f)=o(1)
as
\varrho\to\infty
.

Proof.

In only remains to prove the statement about the values at
x
when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
. Since
u_{\varrho}=\sum_{j}b_{j}u_{j,\varrho}
it suffices to consider the terms
b_{j}u_{j,\varrho}=E_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}
, where
E_{j,\varrho}=b_{j}\psi_{j,\varrho}\in I^{-1}
uniformly and has symbol
e_{j,\varrho}(x,\xi)\in S^{-1}
supported where
|\xi|\geq\varrho
. Then
\varrho E_{j,\varrho}
and
\partial_{x}E_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly in
\varrho\geq 1
. By Proposition
A.6
we have that
u_{j}(x)=F_{j,\varrho}f(x)
depends linearly on
f
, where
F_{j,\varrho}=\mathbb{F}_{j}a_{j}^{-1}\varphi_{j}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}})\Phi_{\delta_{0}}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
.
We only have to prove the continuity, since the factor
\Phi_{\delta_{0}}
in
F_{j,\varrho}
gives compact support.

We have that

\varrho E_{j,\varrho}u(x)=(2\pi)^{-n}\int e^{i(\langle x,\xi\rangle+\phi_{j}(x,\xi))}\varrho e_{j,\varrho}(x,\xi)\widehat{u}(\xi)\,d\xi\qquad\forall\ u\in\mathcal{S}\quad\forall\,x

where
\phi_{j}(x,\xi)
is real and homogeneous of degree
1
in
\xi
and
\varrho e_{j,\varrho}\in S^{0}
uniformly. Thus, we find that

(A.56)

|E_{j,\varrho}u(x)|\lesssim\varrho^{-1}\int_{|\xi|\geq\varrho}|\widehat{u}(\xi)|\,d\xi\leq o(\varrho^{-1})\|\widehat{u}\|_{L^{1}}\qquad\forall\ u\in\mathcal{S}\quad\forall\,x

by dominated convergence as
\varrho\to\infty
, depending only on the seminorms of
e_{j,\varrho}
. This estimate also gives
\partial E_{j,\varrho}u(x)=o(1)
since
\partial_{x}E_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly in
\varrho\geq 1
with symbol supported where
|\xi|\geq\varrho
.
Here
\|\widehat{u}\|_{L^{1}}=\|u\|_{FL^{1}}
is the Fourier
L^{1}
norm, so there exists
C_{n}>0
so that
\|u\|_{FL^{1}}\leq C_{n}\|u\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}
for any
u\in\mathcal{S}
.
Thus, we find that
|E_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}(x)|\lesssim o(\varrho^{-1})\|\Phi u_{j}\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}
and

|\partial E_{j,\varrho}\Phi u_{j}(x)|\leq o(1)\|\Phi u_{j}\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}
,
\varrho\to\infty
,
when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
.
Now
u_{j}(x)=F_{j,\varrho}f(x)
, where
F_{j,\varrho}\in I^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
, so
by Lemma
A.8
we find that

\|\Phi u_{j}\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}\leq C_{0}\|u_{j}\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{\left(\frac{n+1}{2}\right)}\qquad\text{when $\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}$}

which finishes the proof of Proposition
A.12
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
A.1
.

To solve (
A.1
) we may first assume
x_{0}=0
and make the reduction (
A.5
) to the case with vanishing data. Then we find that
f
is replaced by
f_{0}=f-(p_{1}+p_{0}\cdot x)u_{1}-p_{0}u_{0}
,
P_{0}=P
is given by (
A.6
) depending on
u_{1}
and (
A.4
) holds with
u_{1}=0
.
Since we only need local solutions, we shall cut off
\partial v
with
\Phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}
that is supported where
|x|\leq 1
,
0\leq\Phi\leq 1
and
\Phi(x)=1
when
|x|\leq 1/2
.
Starting with
v^{0}\equiv 0
we shall solve the linearized equation

(A.57)

P_{0}(\Phi\partial v^{j}(x,w),x,w,\partial)v^{j+1}(x,w)=f_{0}(x,w)\qquad\forall\,j\geq 0

near
x=0
and
w=w_{0}
with real valued solution
v^{j+1}
such that
\varrho v^{j+1}(x,w_{0})
and
\partial_{x}v^{j+1}(x,w_{0})
are
o(1)
uniformly when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
\varrho\to\infty
depending linearly on
f_{0}
. Thus, for large enough
\varrho
we find that
P_{0}(\Phi\partial v^{j+1}(x,w),x,w,\partial)
is of real principal type when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|\leq c
for some
c>0
.

To microlocalize, we use Propositions
A.4
and
A.12
to find
\varrho_{\delta}\geq 1
and
c_{0}>0
so so that if
\varrho\geq\varrho_{\delta}
,
0<\delta_{0}\leq\min(c_{0}/\varrho,\delta/2)
, we find that (
A.57
) reduces near
x=0
to the coupled system of equations given by (
A.53
) for
j\geq 0
:

(A.58)

Q_{k}(\Phi\partial v^{j}(x),x,D)v_{k}^{j+1}=a_{k}^{-1}\varphi_{k}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}})\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f_{0}\quad\text{$\forall\,k$}

with
v^{0}\equiv 0
. Here
R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}}\in I^{0}
uniformly,
v^{j}(x)=\operatorname{Re}\sum_{k=1}^{N}b_{k}\psi_{k,\varrho}(D)\Phi v^{j}_{k}(x)\in C^{\infty}
for
j>0
with
\varrho b_{k}\psi_{k,\varrho}\in S^{0}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
.
Observe that
a_{k}^{-1}
and
R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}}\in I^{0}
uniformly depending
C^{\infty}
on
\Phi\partial v^{j}(x)
and
w
.

Now
\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(x)=1
when
|x|<\delta_{0}/2
, so by solving (
A.58
) using Proposition
A.6
we will obtain a solution to (
A.57
) when
|x|<\delta_{0}/2
such that
\varrho v^{j+1}_{k}(x,w_{0})
and
\partial_{x}v^{j+1}_{k}(x,w_{0})
are distributions of
f_{0}
which are
o(1)
when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
\varrho\to\infty
.
We are going to prove that the solutions
v^{j+1}
to (
A.57
) are uniformly bounded in
C^{\infty}
near
x=0
, then we can use the Arzela-Ascoli theorem to get convergence of a subsequence to a solution to the nonlinear equation (
A.6
).
Observe that we may solve (
A.58
) in any neighborhood of
x=0
, but this system will only give a solution to (
A.57
) when
|x|<\delta_{0}/2
where
P_{0}
is of principal type.

First we obtain from Lemmas
A.8
and Remark
A.9
that there exists
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
so that for any
m\in\mathbf{N}
there exists
C_{m}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.59)

\|a_{k}^{-1}\varphi_{k}(\operatorname{Id}+R_{\varrho,\delta_{0}})\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f_{0}\|^{2}_{(m)}\leq C_{m}(\|\Phi\partial v^{j}\|_{(\ell)})\|f_{0}\|^{2}_{(m)}

since the operators are in
I^{0}
uniformly depending
C^{\infty}
on
\Phi\partial v^{j}(x)
.

By (
A.58
), (
A.59
) and Proposition
A.10
we also find that
there exists
\ell\in\mathbf{N}
such that for any
m\in\mathbf{N}
there exists
C_{m}(t)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}_{+})
so that

(A.60)

\|\Phi v_{k}^{j+1}\|_{(m)}^{2}\leq C_{m}(\|\Phi\partial v^{j}\|_{(\ell)})\|f_{0}\|_{(m)}^{2}\qquad\forall\,k

since
\Phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}
has support when
|x|\leq 1
.
Here we may assume that
C_{m}(t)
is a nondecreasing function
\forall\,m
.
Now
\varrho\partial b_{k}\psi_{k,\varrho}\in\Psi^{1}
uniformly when
\varrho\geq 1
and
\|\operatorname{Re}u\|_{(m)}\leq\|u\|_{(m)}
for
u\in\mathcal{S}
, which gives

(A.61)

\|\Phi\partial v^{j}\|^{2}_{(m)}\leq\varrho^{-2}\widetilde{C}_{m}\sum_{1\leq k\leq N}\|\Phi v^{j}_{k}\|^{2}_{(m+1)}\qquad\forall\,m\in\mathbf{N}\quad\forall\,\varrho\geq 1

By using (
A.61
) for
m=\ell
and changing
\ell
we find for any
k
that

(A.62)

\|\Phi v_{k}^{j+1}\|_{(m)}^{2}\leq C_{m}\left(\varrho^{-1}\sqrt{\widetilde{C}_{\ell}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\|\Phi v^{j}_{k}\|_{(\ell)}\right)\|f_{0}\|_{(m)}^{2}\qquad\forall\,m\in\mathbf{N}\quad\forall\,\varrho\geq 1

Thus, for any
m\in\mathbf{N}
we will obtain uniform bounds on
\|\Phi v_{k}^{j}\|_{(m)}
if we have uniform bounds when
m=\ell
. Since
v^{0}=0,\ \forall\,k,
we find by taking
m=\ell
in (
A.60
) that

(A.63)

\|\Phi v_{k}^{0}\|_{(\ell)}^{2}\leq C_{\ell}(0)\|f_{0}\|_{(\ell)}^{2}\qquad\forall\,k

where
C_{\ell}(0)\leq C_{\ell}(1)
since
C_{\ell}(t)
is nondecreasing. If we assume for some
j\geq 0
that

(A.64)

\|\Phi v_{k}^{j}\|_{(\ell)}^{2}\leq C_{\ell}(1)\|f_{0}\|_{(\ell)}^{2}\qquad\forall\,k

then by choosing
\varrho\geq N\sqrt{\widetilde{C}_{\ell}C_{\ell}(1)}\|f_{0}\|_{(\ell)}=\varrho_{0}
we find using (
A.62
) with
m=\ell
that (
A.64
) holds with
j
replaced by
j+1
. Since this is true for
j=0
we obtain by induction that (
A.64
) holds for any
j
. By (
A.62
) we obtain for any
m
uniform bounds on
\|\Phi v_{k}^{j}\|_{(m)}
for any
j,\,k
, which by (
A.61
) gives
that
\|\Phi v^{j}\|_{(m)}
is uniformly bounded for any
j
.
This also holds for
|w-w_{0}|<c
for some
c>0
.

By the Arzela-Ascoli theorem there exists a subsequence
\{v^{j_{k}}\}_{j_{k}}
that converges in
C^{\infty}
to a real valued limit
v
on
\Phi^{-1}(1)
, i.e., when
|x|\leq 1/2
, as
j_{k}\to\infty
. By taking the limit of the equation (
A.57
) we find by continuity for large enough
\varrho
that

(A.65)

P_{0}(\partial v(x,w),x,w,\partial)v(x,w)=f_{0}(x,w)

when
|x|<\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
.
We also obtain that

v(x,w)=c_{x,\varrho}(f_{0})\in\mathbf{R}
and
\partial_{x}v(x,w)=d_{x,\varrho}(f_{0})\in\mathbf{R}^{n}
where
\varrho c_{x,\varrho}
and
d_{x,\varrho}\in\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
are
o(1)
uniformly for
\varrho\to\infty
when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
. Thus, for
\varrho\geq\varrho_{1}
large enough, we find that
P_{0}(\Phi\partial v(x,w),x,w,\partial)
is of principal type when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
.

Thus, if
\varrho\geq\max(\varrho_{0},\varrho_{1},\varrho_{\delta})
we have a solution to (
A.65
) when
|x|<\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
with

f
replaced by
\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f_{0}=\Phi_{\delta_{0}}\left(f-(p_{1}+p_{0}x)\cdot u_{1}-p_{0}u_{0}\right)
by (
A.6
) and

v
replaced by
u=v+u_{0}+u_{1}\cdot x
by (
A.5
) so that
P_{0}(\Phi\partial u(x,w),x,w,\partial)
is of principal type when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
.
By linearity we obtain that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u(0,w_{0})=u_{0}+c_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f)-c_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}p_{0})u_{0}-c_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(p_{1}+p_{0}x))\cdot u_{1}\\
&\partial_{x}u(0,w_{0})=u_{1}+d_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}f)-d_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}p_{0})u_{0}-d_{\varrho}(\Phi_{\delta_{0}}(p_{1}+p_{0}x))\cdot u_{1}\end{aligned}\right.

If we replace
u_{j}
with indeterminate
v_{j}
for
j=1,\,2
, then we obtain the linear system

(A.66)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u(0,w_{0})=(1+o(\varrho^{-1})a_{\varrho})v_{0}+o(\varrho^{-1})b_{\varrho}\cdot v_{1}+o(\varrho^{-1})c_{\varrho}=u_{0}\\
&\partial_{x}u(0,v_{0})=o(1)d_{\varrho}v_{0}+(\operatorname{Id}_{n}+o(1)e_{\varrho})v_{1}+o(1)f_{\varrho}=u_{1}\end{aligned}\right.

where the coefficients
a_{\varrho},\dots,f_{\varrho}
are uniformly bounded when
\varrho\geq 1
. Observe that
b_{\varrho}
is a
1\times n
,
d_{\varrho}
and
f_{\varrho}
are
n\times 1
and
e_{\varrho}
is an
n\times n
matrix.
This is a linear
(n+1)\times(n+1)
system in
(v_{0},v_{1})
which converges to the identity when
\varrho\to\infty
.
Thus, there exists
\varrho_{2}\geq 1
so that the determinant of the system is nonvanishing when
\varrho\geq\varrho_{2}
which gives a unique solution
(v_{0},v_{1})
to (
A.66
) so that
|v_{j}-u_{j}|\leq o(1)
,
j=1
, 2, as
\varrho\to\infty
.
By solving (
A.6
) for
\varrho\geq\max(\varrho_{0},\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2},\varrho_{\delta})
large enough with
u_{j}
replaced by
v_{j}
,
j=1,\,2
, we obtain a solution to (
A.1
) when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
. In fact, if
\varrho
is large enough then
P_{0}(\Phi\partial u(x,w),x,w,\partial)
is of principal type when
|x|\leq\delta_{0}/2
and
|w-w_{0}|<c
. This finishes the proof of Theorem
A.1
.
∎
